# CPT Studio — accuracy residuals (routing wording it does not recognise)

Routing reads declared wording, layouts and check boxes. Four refutation rounds each found new phrasings it misreads,
in both directions, and the count did not fall (47, 49, 71, 99 confirmed). So these misreadings are recorded and
measured instead of chased one phrasing at a time (round 4, 2026-10-24):

- **Nothing routing decides is final without a reviewer.** A status exclusion is a recommendation the reviewer must
  confirm or reverse before a reviewed report exists, and approving a code is the reviewer's statement that the whole
  document, not only the cited passages, supports it (ARCHITECTURE truth rule 5 and 5.7). The UI and every report say
  that the service status is not verified.
- **What is still fixed in code:** misreadings of what the code DECLARES it reads (its box vocabulary, its listed words
  and layouts, the form-field contract), regressions, cost and memory, and anything that would let a code be approved or
  removed without a reviewer decision.
- **What is listed here:** wording OUTSIDE every declared list. Each entry is input for the accuracy evaluation the owner
  directive calls for (independently adjudicated cases, withheld from development), where its real frequency decides
  whether it earns a rule.

"Effect now" is the behaviour on the repair branch when the entry was recorded (round 4, round 5, round 4c or round 4d). *Held* means needs_clarification; *ready* means approvable
with the whole-document acknowledgement; *excluded* means a recommendation the reviewer must confirm or reinstate.

| id (round 4) | example input | effect now | why it is not a rule yet |
|---|---|---|---|

## Known limits recorded after repair round 4d (the owner's decision of 2026-10-25)

Refutation round 6 confirmed 59 defects; repair round 4d fixed its 40 blocking findings and what its own verifiers
confirmed in the must-fix and declared classes. By the owner's decision (ruling file, "Owner decision, 2026-10-25"),
after the final round a confirmed misreading outside the must-fix classes - a request sent to Corti that should not
be sent, stage time or memory limits, a report or export that misstates persisted state, a code approved or removed
from the decision set without a reviewer's decision, data loss, security - is a known limit: it is listed here and in
the PR body, and it does not block. These rows include misreadings of DECLARED wording and accepted over-holds, which
the list above did not; each was measured on the integrated round-4d head. *Held*, *ready* and *excluded* mean what
they mean above; "the base" is round 6's head (aaf517e88) unless a row names another tree.

Round 7 found this entry's earlier figures wrong: 20,000 characters of "EUS, " took 22 s, not 50,000 in 40 s, and the stage overran on memory from about 40,000 characters, not past 70,000. So the overrun became a documented bound (settings.MAX_SERVICE_MENTIONS) instead of a stage limit; a real report names its procedures a few dozen times |

## Known limits recorded after refutation round 7 (the final round)

Refutation round 7 attacked the integrated round-4d head (06eddf07d) with eight lenses, and an independent verifier
reproduced every finding below on that head. Its confirmed findings in a must-fix class were repaired, as the owner's
decision of 2026-10-25 requires: two reports that misstated persisted state (the invalidation reason and a refused
provider response) and nine stage time or memory limits (ARCHITECTURE 5.3, 5.4 and 5.6). Those repairs came after the
final round, so no refutation pass read them; each was instead shown exact or hold-only by a differential over the
corpora and proved by mutation. Every other confirmed misreading is listed here and in the PR body, and does not block.
None of these rows was fixed after the final round: each fix would change what a reader reads with nothing to refute
it, which is the risk the rounds existed to catch. "Read correctly by" names the earlier tree that did; "every base"
means aaf517e88, 9a9f403, 4347acd and d05326ef.

| id | example input | effect now | why it is not a rule yet |
|---|---|---|---|

The same padding as ordinary spaces in Helvetica is held ("[?] MAC"); read correctly by d05326ef | a string counts as ink unless its text is ASCII spaces, so padding drawn as strings of CID spaces (an Identity-H font) or of no-break spaces is never seen as a column gap; the fix (decide it from the glyphs' mapped characters, as the trim already does) was not made after the final round |

The same indent in ASCII spaces is held; 9a9f403 held the form-XObject page | the placement skips a leading string only when its text is ASCII spaces; not fixed after the final round |

## Refutation of the GI core, 2026-10-27: known limits

The GI-core change (engine @4: the documented-service reader, reconciliation, completeness and the bounded second
review; ARCHITECTURE 5.9-5.12) was attacked by refutation round r1 (ten lenses, 96 findings: verdicts at 8f8a3b48,
re-checks at 9086a4271), then repaired in round ec5 (clusters SENDS, CONTEXT, REPORTRUNS, REPORTFACTS, COST, EXCL and
FORMATS, each attacked by its own skeptic) and round 7c8 (a skeptic and a fix round per cluster, CONTEXT rounds 2 and 3,
and refutation phase 1 over b611b557a with its fix round P1SENDS, P1REPORTS and P1INSTALL). By the owner's decision of
2026-10-27 (narrow, install-first; ruling file, "Owner decision, 2026-10-27 (session ec5e7910)") only the must-fix
classes were repaired: a request sent to Corti that should not be sent (a wrong or conflicting date, an identical
re-send), stage time or memory limits, a report, export or screen that misstates persisted state, a code approved or
removed from the decision set without a reviewer's decision, data loss, rollback safety and security. Every other
confirmed misreading is listed here and in the PR body, and does not block: the reviewer decides every code (READY is
advisory and an approval carries the whole-document acknowledgement; an exclusion is a recommendation the reviewer
confirms or reinstates before a reviewed report exists), and since 8985dde42 no second review is sent unless a workspace
owner or reviewer asks for it, so a misreading that once paid for an automatic second review now at most offers one.

This table accounts for all 96 r1 findings - a repaired one names the commit whose message names it - and for every
skeptic or refutation issue of rounds ec5 and 7c8 (refutation phase 1 included) that no commit repairs. Refutation
phase 2 is still running on the final head; its known limits are appended after this table by the integrator. Effects
use these words: *false READY* (a code is ready for an act the document does not state done today); *over-hold* (held
for the reviewer although the document supports the code); *hidden missed service* (a service or act done today that no
proposal covers and the encounter does not list as unresolved, so coding can read complete); *phantom unresolved item*
(a service or act the document does not state done today, listed as unresolved; *offered for a second review* when the
item is marked for one, so a reviewer's ask would send it); *capability loss* (something a member could do before, they
no longer can). "Measured at 2fc8cde3d" means the finding's own input, or its re-check's smallest input, was run for
this record on a copy of 2fc8cde3d with synthetic text only; "verified in code" means the statement was read from the
code at 2fc8cde3d; "not re-measured at 2fc8cde3d" names the last tree that measured it. *Held*, *ready* and *excluded*
mean what they mean above.

"On withdrawal, no polyp was snared." and "Per patient preference, no lesion was biopsied." ARCHITECTURE 5.11.) | a capability loss; nothing is sent and no code is approved or removed |

How an entry leaves this list: the accuracy evaluation shows the phrasing occurs in real adjudicated documents, and a
rule is written for it under the hold-only rule (it may hold; it never newly excludes or releases), with a corpus
differential showing no release.

### Refutation phase 2 (2026-10-28): known limits

Refutation phase 2 attacked the integrated heads 20568b077 and 2fc8cde3d (lenses sends2, reports2, install2, excl2,
cost2, formats2, then dates, ctxcost and interplay), each finding re-run by an independent verifier. Its must-fix
findings were repaired (stage cost: cost2 0-2 and ctxcost 0-1; report truth: reports2 0-3; rollback: install2 1-2; the
service date sent: dates 0-3, 5 and interplay 0, and dates 8's brackets and words after the date), then attacked again:
each repair by a skeptic and one fix round (P2DN, P2REPORTS, P2INSTALL), the fix rounds on the integrated head (P2RA),
the final refutation of those (FR, on c3c034a08: 15 confirmed must-fix, repaired) and a differential check of that
last fix round against c3c034a08. The service date's remaining dates 4, 6, 7 and 8 shapes are the qualifier family
below (row p2 dates-q), open for the owner (ruled 2026-10-28: the narrow source rule; see that row's note and "GI round
2" below). The rows below are the confirmed findings that are not must-fix, or not
repaired. The
exclusion rows are graded by interpretation 27 (ruling file, 2026-10-28): every status exclusion is reinstatable and
no reviewed report exists until the reviewer confirms it or reinstates the code, so a misreading that recommends
excluding an act done today is a wrong recommendation the reviewer decides. *Excluded (reinstatable)* below means that
recommendation; each row was measured by its verifier on a copy of the head named, with synthetic text only.

| id | misreading or limit | effect | why it is not must-fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| p2 excl2-0 | once the exclusion guard reads the page-joined view (FORMATS sk:11, integrated as 0bc8bdd6d), a sentence split across a page join reads as its one-page form does, and both readers misread the joined sentence the same way ("Hemorrhoid banding was performed today, the surgical consult" / "having been cancelled."; "... today, six weeks after the first" / "banding on 09/02/2026.") | measured at 20568b077: excluded (reinstatable) in the two-page PDF, as the one-page text already was; held at 164a9c6eb only because the guard did not read the join; its verifier graded it no defect (the PDF now reads as the same words on one page) | the two-page reading now equals the one-page one (format parity); the exclusion is a recommendation the reviewer confirms or reinstates (interpretation 27) |
| p2 excl2-1 | another event's clause after the date with a predicate the rule does not read ("... today because the polypectomy in 2025 left residual adenoma.", "... because the biopsies in 2025 came back nondiagnostic.") | measured at 20568b077: 45385 / 45380 excluded (reinstatable) as historical; 8 of 15 paraphrases of sk:12's input; pre-existing at 057a7d398 | interpretation 27 |
| p2 excl2-2 | another event's date in a phrase with no predicate of its own ("because of an incomplete resection in 2025", "before the surgery on 11/20/2026", "repeat due 10/2029") | measured at 20568b077 in TXT, DOCX and PDF: excluded (reinstatable) as historical or planned; pre-existing at 057a7d398 | interpretation 27 |
| p2 excl2-3 | a cancel word of another subordinate or absolute predicate in today's act's clause ("because dilation was deferred", "the EUS having been cancelled") | measured at 20568b077: the act's code, or the whole service's, excluded (reinstatable) as cancelled; pre-existing | interpretation 27 |
| p2 excl2-4 | an office visit whose only status reading is routing's own E/M reading ("Office visit today with review of prior records."): routing's "historical" reaches the documented visit, so the E/M rule of 950be6f4b does not apply | measured at 20568b077: 99213 excluded (reinstatable) as historical on 28 of 36 variants; pre-existing at 057a7d398 (the gi-01/gi-05 class at its reported inputs is repaired) | interpretation 27; the reported gi-01 and gi-05 inputs are repaired, and the reviewer confirms or reinstates every exclusion |
| p2 excl2-5 | "Today, the ..." cut into a clause of its own, so a plan or history word later in the act's clause dates it ("Today, the polyp was removed with a cold snare instead of at the next colonoscopy.") | measured at 20568b077: excluded (reinstatable) on 42 of 60 variants; the same sentences without the comma are ready or held; pre-existing | interpretation 27 |
| p2 excl2-6 | documentation-stamp paraphrases outside the stamp rule ("Addendum written/of/recorded/filed 10/16/2026:", ", per the addendum of 10/16/2026") date the act the addendum adds | measured at 20568b077: excluded (reinstatable) as planned; pre-existing | interpretation 27 |
| p2 excl2-7 | manner-phrase paraphrases outside MANNER_PHRASE ("as was planned", "in line with the plan", "as recommended at the prior colonoscopy") | measured at 20568b077: excluded (reinstatable) as planned or historical; pre-existing | interpretation 27 |
| p2 formats2-0 | the 80-character reach of the other-event rule (OTHER_EVENT_REACH) counts a page join's running header, so the predicate after the date is not found in the PDF | measured at 20568b077: the snare is ready in TXT, DOCX and a two-page PDF without the header, excluded (reinstatable) in the two-page PDF with the patient header | a format difference in a recommendation the reviewer confirms or reinstates (interpretation 27) |
| p2 formats2-1 | continuation furniture at a page join ("continued on next page", "(continued)") is not skipped as a running header, so the joined sentence reads otherwise | measured at 20568b077: four corpus-derived codes ready or held in TXT, DOCX and other PDFs, excluded (reinstatable) in the two-page PDF with the marker | interpretation 27 |
| p2 formats2-2 | a "so" or "but" clause stating the act done today, after a clause about another time or a denial of another site ("The previous banding in 2025 failed, so two rubber bands were placed ... today.", "No biopsies were taken from the duodenum, but four were taken from the gastric antrum.") | measured at 20568b077: excluded (reinstatable) in TXT, DOCX and PDF alike; pre-existing | interpretation 27 |
| p2 sends2-0 | a report with a documented date whose first request never reached Corti (an authentication refusal: not sent) is never attempted again by a metadata save once a member date conflict was held and cleared, or the documented date entered: the not-sent run carrying that request stays the run in use (a held run is skipped by _run_in_use, 4bf9f0e7e) | measured at 20568b077 and b611b557a: one attempt in all, the encounter stays provider_failed; an addendum (a changed document) or a new upload gets coding; the same steps on an undated report attempt it again. (Repaired 2026-10-28 by GI round 2, 84fea27ff: a save that settles a held member date conflict now makes the request whose one attempt conclusively never reached Corti, once per such save and under every send rule; the row was narrower than the defect - any not-sent attempt, a token refusal, the adapter's own deadline or an unreachable coding endpoint, behind a held and settled date; see "GI round 2", row gi2 sends2-0.) | a capability loss, not a send: nothing is sent wrongly and coding stays reachable by an addendum or a new upload; listed for the owner (class b) in the PR body |
| P2RA rollback, a second review that failed or was not sent | after rollback-app, or the failure path of `app`, an encounter whose bounded second review failed (HTTP 500) or was never sent, beside a primary answer that proposed nothing, is put back to the installed revision 057a7d39's own derivation (no_codes), which lists it "No CPT codes proposed" with the blocker "No CPT code was proposed" | measured by the P2RA verifier with 057a7d398's real code (git archive, its web, page and draft TXT on the same rows) | true of the rows: neither request proposed anything; 057a7d39 reads no second review, so it does not name this one. A second review whose outcome is unknown or that was not read yet is named and refused instead (second_review_unread, ops/INSTALL.md), also behind a newer retry that failed or was not sent (P2FR, ROLLBACK 0: every second review made for the answer is read) |

## GI round 2 (2026-10-28/29): repairs and known limits

The owner's directive of 2026-10-28 (ruling file, "Owner directive, 2026-10-28") asked for the shared extent, negation
and date-field defects of the held-out documents ho-01..ho-04 to be repaired from their sources, for elapsed physician
time to be told from a calendar service-date field, for a source-based policy on dates the document marks as another
occurrence's (ruled by the owner the same day: the narrow source rule), for the controlled recovery of conclusively
not-sent requests to be verified, and for the reviewer-requested EUS second review to be exercised. Session 7c8d38da
integrated ten repairs (sends, reports, present, negation, extent, date, conflicts, attribution, occurrence, ops) and
the triage's rule U at b8cc7b5b5, refuted that head with seven lenses (truth, status, ops, attr, sends, dates, extent;
each finding re-run by an independent verifier), closed what they confirmed in a fix round (truth, extent, dates,
status, attr) and added four integrator commits, on branch `fix/cpt-studio-gi-interpretation-20261028` at aec8edf3f. The
ten held-out documents are development documents of this round (interpretation 29): the "after" readings below are
repairs measured on them, never acceptance evidence, and nothing here is an accuracy measure - every expectation is an
author's, provisional, and the reviewer decides every code. "Before" is the installed revision 2b82889f (ee1aa9a46, the round's
first commit, carries its code unchanged), "after" is aec8edf3f, both read offline on synthetic text; the held-out rows replay the 22 live
encounters' stored responses through each tree, with no provider call.

**The differential round and the second fix round (2026-10-29).** A differential round on aec8edf3f asked one question
of the head - is it worse than the installed revision in a must-fix class? - with three lenses (the round's case
batteries re-run on both trees, the must-fix classes, the stage limits) and a verifier for every finding. It confirmed
four must-fix regressions, all from the fix round and never installed: a quadratic reader over a soft-wrapped procedure
paragraph (edges-1, DM-1), an elapsed-time check that read one raw line and no spelled day (edges-2, F-2), and own-date
doubt rules that let a member's historical date be sent (F-1); and one known-limit regression (F-4, ORDER reading
"outside" as an earlier time). A second fix round closed all five, each toward the installed reading (5b125e737 and
bae4e81ac, integrated as e5ea6c627 and 6546d778e). The rows below that it changed say so; "6546d778e" marks a reading
measured at the final head.

| gi2 sends-no-conflict-recovery | a dated report whose first primary attempt is recorded not sent (a token refusal) with no member date conflict; the member saves a setting, a billing context or the documented date | each save only re-checks with the not-sent run; the encounter stays provider_failed; recovery only by an addendum, a new upload, or holding and then settling a date conflict | round 3 pipeline3:0 pins that a save leaving the request unchanged never re-sends; an explicit member-only "make the request again" for runs that never left is an owner decision (class a) |
| gi2 sends-after-reconcile | a primary outcome unknown, then reconciled; the released attempt recorded not sent; then a member date conflict held and settled | the settling save makes one more attempt: at most one request reaches Corti after the reconciliation (log: unknown, not_sent, succeeded) | a reconciliation releases one request that reaches Corti; if the owner reads "one new attempt" literally this exceeds it: an owner interpretation |
| gi2 sends-repeat-settles | a not-sent primary attempt; the member enters a conflicting date and clears it, again and again, each attempt ending not sent | one attempt per settling save, none behind any other save; once one reaches Corti its outcome stands (a success reused, a failure never re-sent, an unknown blocking) | each attempt answers an explicit member action and conclusively was no submission; nothing is automatic |
| gi2 sends-settle-then-stop | a settling save queues the owed predict; the member presses Stop before it runs, then saves a setting instead of Resume | the setting save only re-checks, so the owed attempt is no longer queued; Resume (before such a save) or another settle makes it | only a settling save re-attempts, the scope the task set; a Stop followed by another save is the member's choice |

### Interpretation 28 corrected by measurement

Interpretation 28 (ruling file, 2026-10-28) says "nothing is sent that the installed revision would not send". Read
against the broad case corpus of 164,585 texts (the constructed case files of every date round plus the existing texts;
the differential round's harness dd.py and compare.py), the sentence was literally false for the previous install:
2b82889f sent a labelled date in 677 constructed texts where 057a7d398 asked (0 existing texts). The triage of those 677
(session 7c8d38da) found 522 wrong or uncertain (the rule U shape: an addendum's own date sent as the header's
other-occurrence procedure's), 5 uncertain (S-blk: a heading block holding both dates) and 150 correct, the note's own
date of service (by the triage's shape labels: S-sib 100, the own label beside a
sibling label the installed reader reads as another occurrence's; M 35, where 057a7d398 read no date at all; S-add 10,
the other occurrence's label only in an addendum after the own one; U-x 3, rule U's shape where an exemption holds;
S-both 2, mixed). The measured invariants (the integrator, dd.py and compare.py over the same 164,585
texts):

- against the installed 2b82889f (ee1aa9a46 carries its code), at 6546d778e: one document date is newly sent -
  df-v-df-d-3:beedaf66bbba, 2026-10-22, the ho-04 time-statement shape, which 057a7d398 sends too; 6 member outcomes
  are newly taken - the texts of row gi2 occ-member-line, where the head asks the outside colonoscopy's date that
  2b82889f sends and the member's date equals the note's own dated title; 7,004 texts are newly asked and 9,671
  member outcomes newly held; and nothing changes on the 22 live, the 10 held-out, the 43 corpus, the 10
  verification and the 8,446 gate texts except ho-04. (At aec8edf3f: 43 member outcomes, 37 more of them through
  the own-dates-in-doubt rules, which the integrator had accepted as each the note's own dated service line; the
  differential round showed some were another, earlier service's line, and the second fix round holds all 37 -
  row gi2 fx2 doubt answers.)
- against 057a7d398, at 6546d778e: 133 texts send a document date 057a7d398 asks (130 at aec8edf3f; the 3 added are
  F-4's place-word texts, which 2b82889f sends), all inside the triage's 150 shapes (S-sib 87, M 32, S-add 10, U-x 2,
  S-both 2); read member by member, 131 are the note's own date by the source and two are not (row gi2 dates-F1-n1,
  which 2b82889f sends too); the other 17 of the 150 are asked by BIRTH, PART and ORDER; no member outcome differs.

So the 527 wrong or uncertain sends are asked, never sent, and the sentence now holds but for those 133, which the
integrator accepted by decision (row gi2 dates-F1; the owner may overrule), two of them wrong by the source and
listed for the owner. The ruling file keeps interpretation 28 as written; this is its measured correction
(interpretation 37 corrects interpretation 31's figures the same way).

## GI round 3 (2026-10-29/30): the narrow release

The owner's directive of 2026-10-29 (ruling file, "Owner directive, 2026-10-29") asked for repairs to four things:
- the shared date and section binding, h2-08 first;
- the extent and table interpretation;
- the current-visit versus future-follow-up scope;
- the inventory's fact binding.

Session 7c8d38da ran four investigations, four repairs and two fix rounds on branch
`fix/cpt-studio-gi-binding-20261029`. Six independent lenses refuted each round, finding 45, 46 and 46 findings. The
third was a differential round, with 41 findings blocking. Each fix round fixed the previous round's findings and
introduced new ones: new date sends, a member's correct answer refused, quadratic shapes, and decisions lost. On
2026-10-30 the owner decided to ship a narrow release instead (ruling file, "Owner decision, 2026-10-30").

That branch, with its refutations' findings (session scratch, summarised in the ruling file), is kept as the next
round's starting record. None of its rules ship. Nothing here is an accuracy measure: every expectation is a source
review of synthetic text, and the reviewer decides every code.

### What ships (`fix/cpt-studio-gi3-narrow-20261030`, built on 90377ec6f413)

| id | the defect (input) | before (90377ec6f413) → after | how |
|---|---|---|---|
| gi3 h2-08 | h2-08: the header's "Date of service: 2026-10-05"; at the end of its "Assessment and plan", "Charge information" / "Date of service: 2026-10-06" | 2026-10-06 dropped (rule A skips every plan-section date label) and 2026-10-05 sent → the date is asked and the request is sent with no date line. The question says only what is true of the document: "The lines from “Charge information” to the document's end state “Date of service: 2026-10-06”, while 2026-10-05 is read from “Date of service: 2026-10-05”". A member's 2026-10-05 makes one dated request. Any other answer is held with the installed note ("... the document gives 2026-10-05 ..."): every answer is taken or held exactly as installed takes or holds it. The block may record this encounter or another occurrence, so its date is never taken (the narrow refutations) | only where the installed reading sends a date (`context._charge_block_asks`): the note's own closing charge block states another date of service (`_closing_charge_block`, read once per document). The block must:<br>- close the document;<br>- sit in a plan section's reach;<br>- be headed by a heading of the closed class `CHARGE_HEADINGS`, alone or joined to its first field;<br>- hold only fields, code or cell lines and signature lines, none over 120 characters;<br>- name no other occurrence on any of its lines (`_CHARGE_ELSEWHERE`);<br>- not belong to a copied note (`_block_in_another_note`: no heading-like line naming another occurrence between the block and the document's first plan section, or on the nearest non-empty line above it);<br>- carry no date of birth.<br>Only the encounter's own date-of-service label counts (date of service, DOS, service or encounter date). The document's own date settles it in a plan's reach or in the application's addendum section; a line beyond both, a running footer after a section-opening field among them, settles nothing. The block runs from its heading to the document's end, so an entry written after the signature is among its lines. The question keeps the installed fact's evidence only, so only the document's own date is a member's answer; what the lines state is named in its note only, each stated line quoted whole, as theirs and never as the block's (`CHARGE_DATE_NOTE`, read by `charge_date_problem`). Every reader reads the date the installed reading sends (`context.service_date_of`, `charge_question_day`, `engine.assess`), so the document reads exactly as installed reads it, unanswered or with a member's answer held over a kept provider answer; only the gates keep no date (each proposal holds on the question, and no dated reference applies). The report, the screen, the list and the change reason say "asked", never "conflicting". Every fact the installed reading does not send - no own date, or a question it already asks - is returned exactly as it made it, so nothing is newly sent, not even through a member's answer |
| gi3 h2-08's layouts | the charge block with a CPT or ICD line, field lines, a cell table, a heading in bold ("**Charge information**", "__Charge information__"), the heading joined to its first field by a colon or a dash, with or without blanks ("Charge Information: Date of Service: ...", "Charge Information — Date of Service: ...", "Charge Information—Date of Service: ...", "Charges: 99214, date of service ..."), a dash bullet before a dash-joined heading ("- Charge information - Date of service: ..."), a field that opens with a signature word ("Signed ABN on file: no", "Dictated by: ..."), or a signature line after it (the second refutation's F7, and the narrow refutations' dates-n1, dates-n2-8, dates-n4-3, dates-n5-2 and dates-n5-3 shapes) | 2026-10-05 sent → asked, as above | the same rule |
| gi3 context re-read | a context 90377ec6f413 recorded (gi-context@2), such as the live e084b793 (the earlier h2-08), which says 2026-10-05 as documented | kept current, so a predict queued or stopped at the install, a reconciliation or a second review would send 2026-10-05 again → remade under the current rules before any stage sends or assesses on it, and no second review is offered on it | `jobs.CONTEXT_VERSION` gi-context@3, with @1 and @2 earlier (interpretation 49) |
| gi3 STATE2-1 | sending disabled; a pre-install encounter whose context is remade by a resumed assess, or by the stale-stage sweep with no member action | assessed with no provider answer: every proposal dropped, the reviewer's decisions invalidated, and an unknown or failed outcome shown as "provider not run" → an unchanged request re-assesses with the recorded answer. When the request changed, an unknown outcome blocks the upgrade's predict (the encounter reads provider unknown), and any other recorded request stops it, keeping what is recorded. With no request recorded it runs as before | `jobs._request_for` (the adapter's own request builder) and `jobs._recorded_in_use` |

### Measured against the installed revision (offline, synthetic; never an accuracy figure)
Measured on b5ddc4d32, whose `cpt-studio/` code and requirements are what ship. Later commits change documentation,
and the merge of `main` adds only the rebuild's pypdf pin and its documents (PR #1449).
- Full suite: 3,585 Django tests OK (3 skipped); ops 211 and 37; node 61 and 56; no migration.
- Reading gate: reader 0 changes in 8,446 texts; engine 0 changes in 6,397 stored assessments (no stored text reads the
  question).
- The 36 live encounters' stored responses, replayed: one changes. h2-08's date is now asked, and its check reads "The lines
  from “Charge information” to the document's end state “Date of service: 2026-10-06”, while 2026-10-05 is read from “Date
  of service: 2026-10-05”". No status line moves.
- A mechanical differential over 227,700 constructed date texts, field by field against the installed reading:
  - 225,540 are identical in every recorded field (the fact, its note, setting, status, evidence, the date routing reads,
    and whether each date the text states, and a date none states, is taken or held as a member's answer);
  - 2,160 are the installed reading's sent date turned into this release's question, with the same evidence, setting,
    status and routing date, and every member answer taken or held exactly as installed takes or holds it;
  - 0 are anything else, so none is newly sent and no member answer changes.
- The assess stage's own child over 42,883 texts in three member states, against installed, with fee-schedule rows
  loaded and one synthetic provider answer of four codes: 7,486 constructed, 6,545 real documents with a closing block
  appended so the release asks them, and 28,852 of the sixth refutation's inputs:
  - routing is identical in every state;
  - the document's own answer gives installed's assessment in every recorded field;
  - an open question equals installed's assessment with the date in every proposal's bucket, exclusion reason and rationale
    and in every encounter check and question, except the date check, the date question and the completeness line.
    Installed's 3,911 ready proposals hold on the question, none is ready, and the excluded set is exactly installed's;
  - a held answer over a kept provider answer reads as the open question does, and a date held with no provider answer
    is identical to installed's on all 5,775 held uploads of the eighth refutation's scan;
  - the controls fail as they must: the tree before the routing repair breaks routing on 156 of the real documents, the
    tree before the readers' repair breaks the open question's equality on 51, and the tree before the held repair breaks
    the held equality on 51. That corpus holds no performed XXX service, so it cannot see parity-7-2: its evidence is the
    next line, a test and a mutant.
- The whole result, every leaf, over the seventh refutation's sets (1,120 texts, 3,820 open questions, its office set with
  XXX codes among them): every difference is the date, the date question, the completeness line, a ready proposal held, a
  dated reference left unknown, a fee-schedule row's sentence not shown, an excluded code's reinstatement waiting on the
  date, or a documented service's date field. Each is named in "gi3 what an open question holds".
- The question's sentence, checked literally on all 42,883 texts of that set, 0 violations: its first quote is a line
  of the document, each stated quote is that line or a whole line after it, and its own quote stands in the document.
  Planted violations fail. The test helper makes the same check on every asked text in its module.
- Cost: the context stage is linear to the 400,000-character cap over seventeen adversarial shapes (doubling ratios about
  2). The worst is 2.70 s (blank lines after the block), against 2.26 s for installed on the same text. The eighth
  refutation's bounded assess child: worst 11.8 s against installed's 11.1 s on one shape, 8.8 s against 4.9 s on another,
  and 136 MiB in both, under limits of 120 s and 768 MiB, on the commit before the last repair.
- Mutants: 61 of 61 killed on a green baseline, each restore checked by sha256: the reader and gate split, the note's literal
  claims, the labels, the heading readers, the charge-block guards and the state safeguards. Four survivors along the way
  were findings: a dead bound, removed; the region's lower bound and the evidence readers' date, each now pinned by a test;
  and an addendum test that used a header the application does not write, rewritten on its own.
- Refutations of the narrow diff, each by independent lenses told to refute:
  - the first, on 5a2c36b9a (dates, state, cost), found dates-n1 to n4, STATE-N1 and STATE-N2. All are repaired except
    dates-n2, a charge date with words after it, which is a known limit below;
  - the second, on a38aee190 (dates, state, cost), found seven shapes in which another occurrence's closing charge
    block passed the guards and its date was taken as a member's answer and sent (dates-n2-1 to n2-7), dates-n2-8 (a
    dash-joined heading, now read) and STATE2N-1 (repaired); cost found nothing. The block's date stopped being an
    answer at all (interpretation 53(b));
  - the third, on a52a1a964 (dates, state), found no send and no wrong answer, but texts that quoted the block's date
    as a documented date of service (dates-n3-1 to n3-3, STATE3N-W1). The release now changes only a date the
    installed reading sends (interpretation 54). dates-n3-4 and n3-5 are known limits below;
  - the fourth, on 1080eb5ad (dates), found no send. It found that an own date hyphen-joined to its label
    ("DOS-10/05/2026") could not answer the question (dates-n4-1), and that an entry after the signature was named as
    the block's (dates-n4-2). Both are repaired (interpretation 55), and so is its limit, dates-n4-3 (an underscore-bold
    heading, a dash with no blanks);
  - the fifth, on c833f8b09 (dates), found no send and no answer wrongly taken. Seven signature forms left an entry
    written after the signature in the block, and the question called that entry's date the block's (dates-n5-1); a
    field opening with a signature word cut the block short (dates-n5-3); a dash bullet hid a dash-joined heading
    (dates-n5-2); and routing read with no date on the question (dates-n5-4). All four are repaired (interpretation 56):
    the signature end is gone, the question's text is true by construction, and routing reads the date the installed
    reading sends;
  - the sixth, on e09b69b4c (dates, parity, pipeline), found no wrong date sent and no member answer changed. It found the
    inventory and evidence readers reading with no date, so a 2025 colonoscopy read as done today and a code went from
    ready to excluded (parity-6-1, parity-6-2, pipe6-4); a sentence still asserting what it could not know (dates-n6-1, n6-3
    to n6-6); and other readers calling the fact conflicting (dates-n6-2, pipe6-2, parity-6-3). All are repaired
    (interpretation 57). Pipe6-1, pipe6-5 and parity-6-4 are known limits below; pipe6-3 and pipe6-6 are rollback limits in
    the declaration;
  - the seventh, on 55bf54530 (text, parity, pipeline), was differential: only what is worse than installed, or a text the
    release adds that misstates the source, could block. It found no wrong date sent, and the sentence held literally on
    37,910 asked texts. With fee-schedule rows loaded for the first time, it found that a code with no row counts as
    procedural, so a breath test raised a same-day E/M question (parity-7-2, pipe7-1); that a held answer re-read the kept
    dateless answer with no date, so a code went to excluded on a false reason (parity-7-1, pipe7-2); and that the limit on
    a save reached later encounters too (text-1, pipe7-3). The first two are repaired (interpretation 58), and the limit's row
    now says what is true. An addendum stating the date now settles the question (text-3). Text-2, parity-7-3, parity-7-4
    and parity-7-5 are named in the limits below;
  - the eighth, on b102d2cbf (one lens, differential, over that delta), found nothing that blocks. It raised two limits, and
    both are repaired because each showed a step taken too far (interpretation 59): a running PDF footer repeating the date
    settled the question, so h2-08 with one stopped asking (delta-2), and a date held with no provider answer no longer
    read as installed (delta-1). Its agreement batteries now flip only the application's addenda, 20 of 580, and every text
    that stops asking returns installed's fact;
  - the ninth, on 27c981629 (one lens over those two conditions), found nothing that blocks: over 16,860 reader texts and 40
    plans on real stages, no text the eighth candidate asked stopped asking, each answer is taken or held as installed, and
    no plan sends a date installed never sent. Its three limits corrected this file's wording, and are named below.

### Known limits (each reads exactly as the installed revision reads it, unless its row says it asks)

| id | input | output | why not repaired in this release |
|---|---|---|---|
| gi3 h2-08 family outside the class | a charge block under "Today's appointment", "Billing and scheduling", "Taper schedule", "Follow-up visit, level 4", "Next steps", "Future plan" or "Follow-up"; a charge table with no heading or space-aligned columns; a descriptor line between the heading and the date ("Office visit, established patient"), or a PDF-wrapped field tail; a charge date with words after it ("2026-10-06 (follow-up visit)"); a charge block that does not close the document; a line over 120 characters in the block (a cost bound: the heading reader is quadratic in a long run of marks or spaces); a heading-like line naming another occurrence between the plan's heading and the block ("Pathology pending", "Next colonoscopy in 3 years": read as a copied note's title); a heading joined to its field with no separator ("Charge Information Date of Service: ..." after a PDF line join); the date in a table column under a "Date of service" header cell; a signature line with neither a colon nor a digit ("Reviewed and electronically signed by Synthetic Doctor", or a bare name line); a block line holding a guard word that names no other occurrence ("Body mass index" in an ICD line, "Fee schedule: ..."); an own history heading or a data-reviewed line naming another occurrence ("Prior endoscopy") between the document's first plan section and the block, when a plan-kind section ("Recommendations") comes early or the line stands right above the plan's heading (the third narrow refutation's dates-n3-4 and n3-5) | the header's own date sent and the other dropped, as installed | the release reads only the closed charge-heading class (owner decision 2026-10-30); widening the binding is what three refutation rounds could not make converge |
| gi3 charge blocks of another occurrence, recognised | a copied report's or note's charge block whose title names it (right above its plan, or further up over its own date and history), a booking ("Colonoscopy scheduled ...", "Prior authorization: ..."), a prior visit's late charge, a pathology charge, or a date of birth the reader reads, auto-filled into the charge date (the narrow refutation's dates-n3 and dates-n4) | read as installed: the header's own date is sent | the guards keep the question for the note's own block |
| gi3 charge blocks of another occurrence, not recognised | the second narrow refutation's dates-n2-1 to n2-7: a copied note announced in a sentence, a field or an OCR-misread title; a copy-forward; a booking announced in a sentence; an appended telephone encounter; a late or pathology charge in other words; another patient's block; a date of birth in a form the reader does not read ("DOB: 06/02/59") | asked where installed sent the header's date: an extra question. So the text is true by construction, and this question is the cost |
| gi3 what an open question holds | a document the release asks, unanswered or with a member's answer held over a kept provider answer | every reader reads as installed reads it with the date it sends: routing, the inventory, the evidence readers, the excluded codes and whether a code is a procedure. What waits on the date: no proposal is ready; the fee-schedule, unit and endoscopic-base checks read unknown (the last is absent for a code outside a procedure profile, since only a row names its base); the considerations a fee-schedule row adds (modifier 53, PC/TC) are not shown; an excluded code's reinstatement waits on the date; global days are not verified; a documented service's date field is empty | a date in question cannot open a gate, and a dated reference cannot be applied to it. The answer that takes the date restores installed's assessment exactly |
| gi3 a held answer after the dateless request | a member's other date, 2026-10-06 among them, after the first dateless request was sent (the sixth refutation's pipe6-5 and parity-6-4) | nothing is sent; the dateless answer still answers the held context, so its proposals stay, read as an open question reads them and held on the question. Installed, which had sent a dated request, records a hold and shows none. A date held with no provider answer, at upload, reads exactly as installed | keeping a recorded answer loses nothing, and the document's own answer restores installed's assessment |
| gi3 a save that leaves the question open | a member's save that leaves the question open while the run in use is a dated request: on any encounter, clearing a date; on an encounter recorded before this install, also a save of something else, such as the setting (the sixth refutation's pipe6-1, the seventh's text-1 and pipe7-3) | one dateless request is sent, where installed only re-assessed. Never a date. The re-assessment holds every proposal on the question, so a decision on a ready code is invalidated, with its reason recorded, and does not return when the date is entered again. A failed dated request is followed by this dateless one too. At this install one encounter can be reached by a save of something else, e084b793 (of 36), and it has no decisions | a dateless request is what an open question asks. Avoiding it would pair a dated answer with an undated context, which the stages' date pairing refuses, and that path is where the parked work kept regressing |
| gi3 a dateless request beside a retained dated one | the identical text uploaded again with no date, after a dated request for it is retained in the workspace (the seventh refutation's text-2) | one dateless request is sent, where installed reuses the retained answer | it is a different request from the retained one, so no identical re-send. The duplicate protection holds for the dateless request itself |
| gi3 a decision made while the question stands | a reviewer's decision on a held proposal, then the document's own date as the answer (the seventh refutation's parity-7-5) | invalidated, with its reason recorded, because the proposal's status changes | the existing rule for any status change; the reviewer decides again on the settled reading |
| gi3 lines a guard misjudges | a line that is no heading ("Coding: full code", "billing." ending a wrapped sentence, "99214 - Charge: $150.00") or a label that is no date of service ("Timely filing deadline from date of service: ...", "Posting date, not the date of service: ...") in the closing region (the sixth refutation's dates-n6-4 and n6-5) | asked where installed sent the header's date: an extra question, whose text quotes the lines as they stand | the guards only decide whether to ask, so a wrong guess costs a question and the text stays true |
| gi3 an addendum stating the date | an addendum line stating the document's date, even when its own words retract it ("Date of service: 2026-10-05, changed to 2026-10-06 by billing", the eighth refutation's delta-2) | the question is settled and that date is sent, as installed sends it. Under a field that opens its own section ("Diagnosis: ...", "Findings: ...") it does not settle: the question stays open and a member's 2026-10-05 is still taken | an addendum is the route the question offers, and no guard on its words can read it. The other shape errs toward asking |
| gi3 a footer in a plan's reach | a running footer repeating the document's date with no section-opening field above it, so it stands in the plan's reach (the ninth refutation) | agrees, so the block's date stops asking and the header's date is sent, as installed sends it | a plan's own line stating the date agrees, and nothing tells a footer from it there. h2-08 itself has such a field, and asks |
| gi3 the block's date as an answer | a member answers h2-08's question with 2026-10-06, the date its charge block states | held with the installed note ("... the document gives 2026-10-05 ..."); nothing is sent. An addendum stating the date settles it | the block may record this encounter or another occurrence, and no guard can always tell which (the narrow refutations); a date that may be another occurrence's is never a member's answer (the owner's decision of 2026-10-28; interpretations 53 and 54) |
| gi3 history charge block | a billing block in a history section's reach ("Prior procedures:" / "Billing" / "Date of service: ...") | read as installed (dropped) | it may bill a prior occurrence, and asking would offer that occurrence's date as a member's answer (the third refutation's STATE3-1) |
| gi3 unread separators | "Date of service........ 10/05/2026", or a non-breaking space after the colon | not read, as installed | a reader change outside the release |
| gi3 other contradicted dates | the refutation rounds' other class-1 shapes: a later or earlier visit's date near a follow-up title; a planned procedure's date in a clinic note | as installed | interpretation 41: no reliable-coding claim while any is known |
| gi3 STATE-3 | a pre-install encounter on reload (all 36 live encounters are gi-context@2) | shows its recorded reading until a member's action or a stage reads it again; e084b793 still shows 2026-10-05 as documented | a reload runs no stage; nothing sends that date again (the re-read above) |
| gi3 STATE3-5 | sending disabled, and a member explicitly resumes the stopped upgrade predict | assessed with no answer, as installed assesses any predict while sending is disabled | a member's explicit action: enable sending before resuming |

| gi3 extent, scope and inventory | the full round's targets: h2-01, h2-02 and h2-04's extents from tables; h2-07's 99213; the h2-10 stent exchange | as installed | not in the narrow release. The parked branch's repairs measured better on curated corpora, but every refutation found regressions against installed |

### pypdf 6.21.4 was not taken in round 3 (the advisories, 2026-10-31; interpretation 61)

Superseded by the next section, which takes 6.21.4 with the reader repaired. This one is kept as written: it is
the record of the refutation the repair answers, row by row.

This release keeps pypdf 6.21.2, as installed. Seven advisories against 6.21.2 (CVE-2026-119203 to
CVE-2026-119209) are denial of service from a crafted PDF. The ingest child contains them: it has an address-space
limit and a CPU limit, and its process group is killed at the deadline. CPT Studio's own workflow audits this
file, so it stays red after its tests pass until CPT Studio moves. The required audit reads the rebuild's pins,
which PR #1449 moved.

The move was refuted before this decision. The candidate was pypdf 6.21.4 plus a reader repair that:
- names a page whose text-layer text holds U+FFFD;
- decodes `_ING_glyphs` as pypdf does;
- labels the reading `cpt-studio-ingest@4`.

That candidate is kept unmerged on branch `prep/cpt-studio-pypdf-6.21.4-20261031` as the next round's start. Its
documentation is known to be wrong where n-6 says.

Four lenses compared it with installed through the real ingest child, and each row recorded both pypdf versions.
An independent agent reproduced every finding below. The author also reproduced the first three rows. Every input
is hand-built unless a producer is named.

| id | input | 6.21.2 (installed) | 6.21.4 with that repair |
|---|---|---|---|
| TXT-2 (block) | a valid PDF in a predefined character map pypdf does not implement, ASCII written as one byte, an odd byte count. ReportLab's CIDFont writes it with KSCms-UHC-H, 90msp-RKSJ-H, EUC-H, B5pc-H and eight more | failed: no usable text layer, so OCR reads the page | ok: a page of CJK mojibake, no OCR, under "they are shown as �". An even byte count already reads as mojibake, unnamed, on 6.21.2 |
| TXT-3 (block) | a check mark whose /Differences glyph name is private-use or a control code (/uniF00C, /uF00C, /uni0001, /uni0000), with no ToUnicode | "[/uniF00C] Cold snare polypectomy": performed | "[] Cold snare polypectomy": an unchecked box, negated, so a ready code is excluded. Through a ToUnicode entry, 6.21.2 already reads "[]" |
| TXT-1, n-1, n-2 (block) | a cut two-byte string, or one-byte text in a two-byte font, in an appearance the reader reads: a stamp, a signature, a push button, a FreeText without /Contents, a field without a value. pypdf's own form filler writes it for a blank field whose subset font lacks a character | the notice (last byte 0x80 or above), or partial: "a form field or annotation whose text could not be read" | the notice is lost, or partial becomes ok, with U+FFFD and mojibake in the labelled block |
| n-4 | a page under 20 readable characters holding U+FFFD, with no OCR result (libcairo writes the ToUnicode shape) | failed, no invalid-text notice | failed, and the notice says characters "are shown as �" beside no text |
| TXT-7, n-3 | a cut string in a font whose ToUnicode has an entry for code FFFD | U+FFFD, named | the replacement becomes that entry's character ("ABX", "45381"), unnamed |
| TXT-8, TXT-9 | glyph names outside the Adobe Glyph List form, or /u names that reach pypdf's existing mis-lookups | the name written | a plausible wrong character, or a box state the page does not show |
| TXT-6 | a /uXXXX name too long for a character, in a Type 1 font's built-in encoding | read | the page unread, even when no text uses the font |
| TXT-4, parser-03, parser-04 | a font 6.21.4 no longer builds: an array or dictionary /BaseEncoding, a string or name in /W | read | every glyph U+FFFD, under a notice that blames the PDF |
| TXT-5 | a page 6.21.2 could not read | that page unread | one page's font fails the whole document |
| parser-01, parser-02 | more than 2,000,000 cross-reference stream entries in total | read | objects in object streams vanish: an annotation and its notice are lost at status ok, or the document fails as damaged |
| parser-05, parser-06 | more than 2,000,000 page-dictionary entries | read, or the too-many-pages message | damaged |
| parser-07 | a memory failure inside a dictionary | the memory message | "damaged" or "stopped unexpectedly" |
| parser-08 | a /DecodeParms that is not a dictionary on an ASCII85, ASCIIHex, RunLength or LZW stream | read | the page is reported damaged, though every byte decodes |
| parser-09, parser-10 | an encryption key length out of range | damaged, or refused | "password-protected", or opened with no notice (failed becomes ok) |
| parser-11 | CCITT data with no /Height | a damage report | none |
| TXT-10, n-5, n-6, n-7 | NUL and U+FFFF decoded from glyph names; U+FFFD reaching the block by the reader's own decode, or literally; the candidate's own sentences and one mocked test | unnamed | unnamed, as installed; the candidate's documentation and its block test say more than they show |

The next round's repair must read each shape no worse than installed before CPT Studio moves. The parser rows need
the reader to tell a dropped section or a hit limit from damage, from pypdf's warnings. The text rows need it to keep
6.21.2's refusals for strings it cannot decode as the font says (TXT-2, n-2), and its literal glyph names where a
decoded one would be dropped (TXT-3).

### pypdf 6.21.4 is taken, with the reader repaired (round 4, 2026-10-31; interpretations 61, 62 and 66)

Its refutation found 16 defects in the reader this section describes; round 2, the section after it, repairs them.
Where the two differ (the unit of unreadable text, the CMaps read, glyph names and encodings, boxes, costs), round 2
decides; this section is kept as written, as the record round 2 answers.

CPT Studio now pins pypdf 6.21.4 (`requirements.in`, `requirements.txt`; the rebuild's pin is unchanged). A new upload
records `{'ingest': 'cpt-studio-ingest@4', 'pypdf': '6.21.4'}`, plus the OCR tools' versions or `'ocr': 'not
available'`. A failure decided outside the reader (the child's memory or unexpected-stop handler, or the parent: a
timeout, a signal) records `{'ingest': 'cpt-studio-ingest@4'}` only, as before. No revision is read again: the extract
stage takes only PENDING revisions (`jobs.py` `stage_extract`), so every stored revision keeps the reading and the
`cpt-studio-ingest@3` label it was made with.

**The dependency audit stays red until this lands.** CPT Studio's own workflow (`.github/workflows/cpt-studio.yml`,
not a required check) ends with `pip_audit` over `cpt-studio/requirements.txt`, which on `main` still pins 6.21.2 and
so fails on the seven advisories. It turns green only when this change is on `main`. The audit itself was not run
for this change: it queries a network service, which this round's rules did not allow.

How every row was judged (interpretation 66). Each shape is built from raw PDF syntax in
`tests/support/pypdf_shapes.py` (synthetic text only; ReportLab for the TXT-2 producer route), read through the real
extraction child under 6.21.2 with the installed reader, under 6.21.4 with the installed reader and under 6.21.4 with
this reader, with OCR unavailable and with stand-in OCR tools, and judged by what the document shows. The inputs of
the refutation lenses no longer existed; their recorded descriptions were rebuilt, and every row reproduced on the
two old columns before the repair was written. `tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf.py` pins each row under 6.21.4; 21
mutations, each reverting one repair on a copy of the tree (applied and restored by sha256), were each killed by those
tests.

The reader's rules (ARCHITECTURE.md 5.1): text pypdf cannot read as the PDF encodes it (a string its CMap cannot
decode, a CMap pypdf does not implement, a font pypdf cannot build or the resources do not name) is never returned
as text: the page's layer is not
read and the page has no usable text layer (OCR, or named), and an appearance showing it is unreadable (named,
partial). Glyph names are read by the Adobe Glyph List specification in the encoding's own precedence. A glyph that
names no character alone in typed brackets is the unread mark `[?]`. U+FFFD in what a page returns is named, and a
failed result carries no such notice.

| id | input | 6.21.2, installed | 6.21.4, installed reader | 6.21.4, this reader | by the source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TXT-1, n-1, n-2 (block) | an appearance showing a cut two-byte string (`<00410042FF>`, `<0041004241>`, a cut 90ms-RKSJ-H or GBK-EUC-H code) or one-byte text in an Identity-H font, in a stamp, a push button, a FreeText without /Contents, a field without a value, a signature, a nested form (16 shapes) | the invalid-text notice at ok (last byte 0x80 or above), else partial: "a form field or annotation whose text could not be read" | ok, `AB�` or mojibake in the labelled block, the notice gone | partial, "a form field or annotation whose text could not be read", nothing of it in the block | **repaired.** The bytes do not encode text in that font, and a cut string cannot be told from one-byte text, so the annotation is unread and named; where 6.21.2 wrote `AB�` at ok it is now partial |
| TXT-2 (block) | ASCII written one byte a character in a predefined CMap pypdf does not implement: KSCms-UHC-H, 90msp-RKSJ-H, 90pv-RKSJ-H, 83pv-RKSJ-H, Add-RKSJ-H, Ext-RKSJ-H, EUC-H, KSC-EUC-H, KSCpc-EUC-H, KSCms-UHC-HW-H, B5pc-H, HKscs-B5-H, CNS-EUC-H, GBKp-EUC-H, UniJIS-UTF8-H; odd and even byte counts; and ReportLab's CIDFont (KSCms-UHC-H, 90msp-RKSJ-H, B5pc-H) | odd: no usable text layer (OCR, or failed); even: CJK mojibake at ok | ok, CJK mojibake and `�`, no OCR | no usable text layer: OCR (method pdf-ocr), or failed and named; odd and even alike. 90ms-RKSJ-H and GBK-EUC-H, which pypdf implements, read their text as before | **repaired**, the even count too (6.21.2 sent mojibake there). The text is read by OCR, never decoded here: no CMap is implemented in this reader |
| TXT-3 (block) | a mark whose /Differences name is private-use or a control code (`uniF00C`, `uF00C`, `uni0001`, `uni0000`) in printed brackets before "Cold snare polypectomy"; and the same mark through a ToUnicode entry U+F00C | `[/uniF00C] Cold snare ...` (the name written; routing read no box); the ToUnicode shape: `[]`, an unchecked box, negated | `[] Cold snare ...`: an unchecked box, negated | `[?] Cold snare ...`, held; the uncounted glyph named, the unread box named, partial | **repaired: never an empty box.** Not read as checked: a private-use or control name says nothing of what the glyph shows (Font Awesome's F00C is a check, its F00D a cross, F096 an empty square), and the box contract holds a mark it cannot read. The 6.21.2 ToUnicode reading, `[]` negated, was wrong and is repaired too. A real check mark name, `uni2713`, reads `[✓]` (6.21.2 wrote the name) |
| TXT-4, parser-03, parser-04 | a /BaseEncoding that is an array, a dictionary or a reference to one (with and without /Differences; also in a ZapfDingbats font); a CID font whose /W holds a literal string, a hex string or a name; a field whose appearance uses such a font | the text as its encoding says (`Polypectomy of a sessile polyp`, `Bssessment`, `❁❂❃❄❅❆❇`, `ABC`); the field shows its value | every glyph `�` at ok; the field reported as differing from its value (partial) | as 6.21.2: the copy extraction reads leaves the entry out | **repaired**, equal to 6.21.2, which pdf.js agrees with |
| TXT-5 | two pages, page 2 in a Type0 font whose /Encoding is a CMap stream or a dictionary (no ToUnicode), with a non-numeric TD or an indirect /W width; and the same font with neither (the pre-existing crash) | partial: page 2 has no usable text layer; the pre-existing shape: the whole document failed, "stopped unexpectedly (TypeError)" | the whole document failed, "stopped unexpectedly (TypeError)" | partial: page 1 read, page 2 has no usable text layer (OCR, or named) | **repaired**, the pre-existing crash too: `pdffonts.kind` no longer raises on a non-name /Encoding, the page's decoding runs inside its guard, and pypdf implements no such CMap, so the page is unread |
| TXT-6 | a Type 1 program whose built-in encoding names a glyph `u` + 20 hex digits; the font used, and unused | the page read (the bad name ignored: `AB`) | the whole page unread (OverflowError in pypdf) | the page read; the bad name stands for no character, counted (`A`, one glyph not extracted); unused: read as 6.21.2 | **repaired.** 6.21.2's `B` was StandardEncoding's guess at a glyph the program names otherwise |
| TXT-7, n-3 | a cut string in an Identity-H font whose ToUnicode maps code FFFD to ☒, X, a space or `ǽ` (by a range) | `AB�` with the notice (last byte 0x80 or above), else no usable text layer | `AB☒`, `ABX`, `AB `, `ABǽ` at ok, unnamed | no usable text layer (OCR, or named) | **repaired** |
| page layer | the same cut strings, and one-byte text, in the page's own content | `AB�` named, or no usable text layer | `AB�` or mojibake at ok, unnamed | no usable text layer; beside a readable page, that page is read and the cut page adds no text | **repaired** |
| TXT-8 | a built-in `u2610` under /WinAnsiEncoding; a built-in `u2612` with /Differences `uni2610`; /Differences `uni0041` or `uni0058` with a ToUnicode entry ☐ for code 0x41 or 0x58 | `A ...`; `/uni2610 ...` (performed); `/uni0041 ...`, `/uni0058 ...` | `☐` (negated), `☒`, `☐`, `☐` | `A`; `☐` (negated: the page shows an empty box); `A`; `X` | **repaired**: /Differences, then the dictionary's named encoding, decide over the program's built-in encoding, and a /Differences character is not looked up again as a ToUnicode code. The pre-existing built-in `uni2610` under WinAnsi (☐ under both versions) now reads `A` too |
| TXT-9 | names outside the specification: `u20`, `u78` in brackets; `u261`, `u0x41`, `u+41`, `u4_1` | the name written (`[/u20]`, `/u261 Biopsy`) | `[ ]` (negated), `[x]`, `ɡ`, `A`, `A`, `A`, unnamed | in brackets `[?]` (held, named, partial); otherwise the glyph is counted and not extracted | **repaired**: such a name stands for no character |
| TXT-10 | `uni0000`, `u0000`, `u0`, `uFFFF` | the name written | NUL dropped unnamed; U+FFFF kept in the text | counted and not extracted, named; noncharacters (U+FFFE, U+FFFF of every plane, U+FDD0 to U+FDEF) count as naming no character wherever they come from | **repaired** |
| n-4 | a page under 20 readable characters holding U+FFFD (a ToUnicode entry, a cut string), no OCR | failed; the cut high byte gave "shown as �" beside no text | failed | failed, no invalid-text notice | **repaired**: a failed result returns no text, so it carries no such notice |
| n-5 | U+FFFD reaching the block: an appearance's ToUnicode, a /V or /RC stream that is not UTF-8, a literal U+FFFD in a value, FreeText /Contents or a field's label | unnamed | unnamed | named: "characters the PDF does not encode as valid text; they are shown as �" | **repaired** |
| n-6, n-7 | the parked candidate's documentation and its mocked block test | | | not taken: this section and ARCHITECTURE.md 5.1 replace them; the tests read real appearances through the child | **repaired** |
| parser-01, parser-02 | an annotation in an object stream reachable only through a cross-reference stream of 2,000,001 entries: a /XRefStm beside a table, or the only section | read in full (3.5 s) | the /XRefStm shape: the annotation and its notice gone at ok (0.4 s); the only-section shape: "damaged" | failed: "its structure goes past a limit this server applies to uploaded files (such as the number of its cross-reference or page entries)" (0.4 s); at exactly 2,000,000 entries it is read in full | **remaining by design**: pypdf 6.21.4 does not read past the limit, so the file is refused and named as past a limit, never read with objects missing and never called damaged |
| parser-05 | 40 pages whose dictionaries hold 52,000 entries each (2,080,200 in all) | all 40 pages read (18 s, 667 MiB) | "damaged" | failed, past a limit (16 s, 625 MiB); 49,000 entries each: read as 6.21.2 | **remaining by design**, named as a limit |

(27 s, 924 MiB) | "damaged" | the same page-count message (counted here from the page tree when pypdf stops at its limit; 26 s, 894 MiB); 41 pages over the limit: "The PDF has 41 pages; the limit is 40." | **repaired**, equal to 6.21.2 |
| parser-07 | a memory failure inside a dictionary (the lens's 1.2 GB object-stream shape) | read (6.21.2 kept a partial dictionary) | "damaged", or "stopped unexpectedly (PdfReadError)" | the memory message, "The document needs more memory to read than the extraction limit allows.", wherever pypdf turns the MemoryError into a read error (at open or later) | **repaired as named, not as read**: 6.21.4 no longer keeps the partial dictionary. The 1.2 GB shape was not rebuilt here; the mapping is pinned by a test of the error chain |
| parser-08 | a /DecodeParms that is not a dictionary (a number, a name, 0, false, an indirect number) on ASCII85, ASCIIHex, RunLength or LZW content, or in a form | read, ok | the same text, "damaged", partial | read, ok; on Flate the same parameter is still named as damage | **repaired**, equal to 6.21.2: those filters take no such parameter and the stream decodes to its end; on Flate a predictor may be lost, as 6.21.2 warned |
| parser-09 | V1/R2 encryption, empty user password, /Length 129 or 1024 | "damaged or not a valid PDF" | "password-protected" (false: there is no password) | "damaged or not a valid PDF" | **equal to 6.21.2**: the file states a key length the standard does not allow, and no empty password opens it here. By ISO 32000-1 Table 20 /Length does not apply to V1, so a reader could open it with a 40-bit key; pypdf does not |
| parser-10 | RC4 with /Length 129 or 41, written with that key length | "damaged or not a valid PDF" | read; the text equals the unencrypted control's | read, as 6.21.4 | **accepted by the source**: the empty password opens it and the text is what the file encodes; 6.21.2 refused it before trying (pdf.js refuses it too) |
| parser-11 | a form, or one of the page's content streams, stored CCITT-encoded with no /Height | "damaged" (partial), or the page unread (failed) | the page's own text, ok | as 6.21.4 | **accepted by the source**: fax data draws nothing as content, and the page shows its own text (pdf.js agrees) |
| pypdf-1 (the parked documentation's limit) | `(...) Tj 0 /A TD (...) Tj` | the page unread | the strings joined, `polypectomyof the sigmoid polyp.` | as 6.21.4 | **accepted by the source**: the invalid operator is ignored, so the second string is drawn right after the first |
| indirect /W width (the parked documentation's other difference) | an Identity-H font whose /W gives the codes the text uses through an indirect number | the page unread: 6.21.2 kept the reference as a width and failed on it | `ABC` | `ABC`, as 6.21.4 | **accepted by the source**: the width is a number, and the text is `ABC` |
| a font the resources do not name (round 4d's `PaddingInsideAStringTests`) | 'MAC' and the next column drawn in `/XX`, which the page's /Font does not hold | the layer read: `[?] MAC` and the column as U+FFFD, at ok, unnamed | the same | no usable text layer (OCR, or named) | **repaired**: pypdf draws such text in its stand-in font, and a viewer draws it in a substitute that OCR reads. That round-4d test now pins this reading (it asserted the ingest@3 one) |
| unbuildable fonts (found here) | a Type0 font whose descendant is not a dictionary; a Type 1 font whose /FontDescriptor is a number | `�` for every byte at ok, unnamed | the same | no usable text layer (OCR, or named) | **repaired**, a defect of both versions: pypdf draws such text in its stand-in font |

Remaining limits, each as it reads on both versions unless said:

| input | output | why not repaired here |
|---|---|---|
| one-byte text in a two-byte font with an even byte count (a stamp's `(Polyp biopsy) Tj` in an Identity-H font) | mojibake at ok, as both 6.21.2 and 6.21.4 read it | the string decodes under the CMap; telling it from real two-byte text needs the ToUnicode's coverage, and refusing every code a ToUnicode lacks would refuse ordinary pages whose subset fonts omit a glyph |
| a /Differences name in the glyph list (`/A`) on a code another ToUnicode entry also keys (`<41>` → ☐) | `☐`: pypdf looks the decoded character up again as a code | pypdf's own double lookup, unchanged by 6.21.4; repairing it means rereading every simple font with a ToUnicode, which no corpus file needed |
| `.notdef` named in a text font's /Differences | `□` (pypdf's glyph list), read as an unchecked box | unchanged by 6.21.4 |
| a private-use mark in brackets (TXT-3) | `[?]`, held | see the TXT-3 row: no reader can tell what such a glyph shows |
| parser-01, parser-02, parser-05 | refused, named as past a limit | pypdf 6.21.4's limits; reading past them is what the advisories exploit |

Ordinary files. Every file in the round's ordinary corpus (123 files of `ordinary.list` and the 10 ReportLab CID files of
its p9 set), the 10 verification documents `ops/verification/generate.py` renders and the 6 regression uploads, 149 in
all, read through the real child: the installed reader on 6.21.2 and this reader on 6.21.4 give the same status,
method, messages, OCR pages and text on all 149, with OCR unavailable and with stand-in OCR. The installed reader on
6.21.4 differs on 6 of them (the p9 KSCms-UHC-H, 90msp-RKSJ-H and B5pc-H files: TXT-2). Time over the 149: 55.7 s
against 55.2 s installed (the longest file 1.31 s against 1.11 s); the child's peak resident memory at most 54 MiB on
either.

Limits and the advisories. `extract.py` and the extraction settings are unchanged: RLIMIT_AS 1536 MiB, RLIMIT_CPU 241 s
soft and 246 s hard, RLIMIT_FSIZE 256 MiB, RLIMIT_NPROC the user's task count plus 64, no core dumps, its own session
and process group, killed at the 240 s deadline. On a live child under 6.21.4 `/proc/<pid>/limits` showed each of
them, and with a deadline set shorter than the document needed the parent killed the group and nothing of it was left.

### Round 2: the refutation of this reader, and its repair (2026-10-31; interpretation 66)

A four-lens refutation of the reader above (`b9d5aa2f1`: wrong text, capability loss, check boxes and forms, cost and
limits) reported 16 findings. Each was reproduced here through the real extraction child, the installed reader
(`origin/main` 06e6d204e, pypdf 6.21.2) beside round 1's (`b9d5aa2f1`, pypdf 6.21.4), on the lenses' own inputs, with OCR
unavailable and with stand-in OCR tools: all 16 reproduce as reported, and all 16 are repaired, each at its cause and in
general. Every repaired reading below is pinned by `tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r2.py`, whose documents are built in
`tests/support/pypdf_shapes.py` (no PDF is committed). The engine label stays `cpt-studio-ingest@4`: round 1's reader
is not on `main` and was never installed.

What the reader does now, where it differs from round 1's rules above (which this replaces where they disagree):

- **The unit of unreadable text is the operator, not the page.** A text-showing operator whose text pypdf cannot read
  as the PDF encodes it is drawn, for extraction, in `pdffonts.unread_font` (codes as wide as its strings, as pypdf
  measures them, so the text around it is spaced as before), dropped, and the page named: "Page N has text in a font or
  encoding this server cannot read as the PDF encodes it; that text was not extracted, so check it against the
  original." (partial). The rest of the page's layer, in fonts pypdf reads as the PDF encodes them, and the page's
  labelled form-field block (read from /V and /AS, without any font) are read; the page goes to OCR only when what
  remains is not a usable text layer, as any page. A font pypdf cannot build is replaced, in what pypdf reads, by that
  font too, so it no longer stops pypdf reading the rest of the page (round 1 and the installed reader lost the page's
  whole layer to it). An annotation's appearance stays one unit: unreadable, named, partial.
- **Text pypdf reads no text from is not judged.** pypdf reads nothing from a form XObject without resources of its own
  (or from a page without any): such text cannot reach the result as U+FFFD or mojibake, so it no longer makes the page
  unread; and the reader no longer gives such a form its parent's resources in the copy pypdf reads (which made pypdf
  read its text in a stand-in font, U+FFFD for every byte, when the page also held a font the reader copies).
- **CMaps.** A UTF-16 CMap pypdf does not list (MuPDF's /UniKS-UTF16-H and -V) is read: pypdf reads every CMap it does
  not implement as UTF-16BE, which is what a UCS-2 or UTF-16 CMap encodes and nothing else does. A composite font
  whose /Encoding is an embedded CMap of two-byte codes only, with a ToUnicode (fpdf2's CID-keyed CFF fonts), is read
  through a copy with the Identity CMap and its widths by code, so its ToUnicode decides every code (pypdf reads such a
  font one byte a code, right only by accident below 0x100).
- **Glyph names and encodings.** A font copied for a glyph name pypdf 6.21.4 reads by its own rule, or whose program
  pypdf cannot read for its built-in encoding, reads every code as the source encodes it (ToUnicode, /Differences, the
  named encoding, the built-in encoding where none replaces it): never pypdf's StandardEncoding guess for a code the
  lost program names, and never a /Differences character looked up again as another code's. /Differences is read as
  pypdf reads it, whole. Glyph names follow the Adobe Glyph List specification's section 2 (a suffix after a period,
  '_' components).
- **Boxes.** NUL (a ToUnicode <0000>, a stored value), an empty ToUnicode destination (copied as the private-use mark)
  and U+FFFD or a lone surrogate alone in typed brackets are the unread mark `[?]` (held, named), in the layer and in
  the labelled block; a field value or note keeps its NUL until the block's guard counts it.
- **A FreeText whose appearance cannot be read** is named as an annotation whose text could not be read, not as one
  whose appearance shows other text than its /Contents (which could not be known).
- **Cost.** `pdffonts.FontReadings`, kept on the document's reader, works out once per font dictionary what pypdf reads
  for it (its copy, whether pypdf builds it, how its strings are judged, its kind), and once per Type 1 program its
  built-in encoding; a string is judged against its font's plan (an even byte count for UTF-16BE). The layer is read
  in one pass that keeps only the text it writes (the box guard holds one position at a time). Every inspection of a
  font is guarded: a font pypdf cannot parse (its structure limits among the reasons) is unusable, its text unread and
  named, never the reason the document fails.

gate passes (the system and migration checks; 3,641 Django tests, 3 skipped; 211 ops; 37 verification; 61
browser-verifier). Seven tests of earlier rounds changed, each with the rule it pins: four of round 1's now read the
operator, not the page, as the unit (the note beside a cut string, or beside a font pypdf cannot build, is read and
the page named); one compares a page whose two strings are dropped without the line break left between them; the
glyph-list test takes 'uni0041.alt' as 'A' (the AGL specification's section 2) and NUL as naming no character; and
`test_repairs_r4d_ingest2`'s column in a font the resources do not hold reads '[?] MAC', the column dropped and
named, as round 4d read it. Each of the 25 mutations, each reverting one repair of this round on a copy of the
tree (applied and checked present, the worktree's files hashed before and after: untouched), was killed by
`tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r2.py` (and, for the operator unit, the page tests of round 1).

| an appearance with one unreadable operator among readable ones | the whole annotation unreadable (named, partial) | the appearance stays one unit, as in round 1; its stored value or /Contents, where it has one, is read |
| a page whose unreadable text is most of it but whose readable rest is a usable layer (20 characters or more) | the readable rest, and the page named; not sent to OCR | the operator is the unit; OCR replaces a page's text (and its block) whole |
| a page left without a usable layer by its unreadable text, with a short labelled block | read with OCR, which replaces its block, as for any page OCR reads | unchanged: an OCR'd page's text is what OCR reads |
| whitespace pypdf writes around a dropped operator | kept (a blank line where a dropped line was, a space) | whitespace; the dropped text is named |
| pypdf's own built-in-encoding override and double lookup in a font the reader does not copy (no 'u' name, a program pypdf reads) | as both versions read it | unchanged by 6.21.4, and no corpus file needed it |
| `.notdef` named in a text font's /Differences | `□` (pypdf's glyph list), read as an unchecked box | unchanged by 6.21.4 |
| a glyph whose ToUnicode gives U+FDD7, the noncharacter the reader writes for text it cannot read | dropped, as text that names no character, but not counted where the page has no unread text | a noncharacter names no character; only its count is lost, on a crafted font |

named as a glyph, and the cost of transcribing a page of unread operators). Each was reproduced here through the real

lenses' own inputs, with OCR unavailable and with stand-in OCR tools: all reproduce as reported. They are repaired
under one rule that ends the churn of rounds 1 and 2: **text that cannot be read is never deleted.** Checking the
repair against that rule found three more readings it did not yet reach (GI-1, RTL-2, N-1 below), each confirmed the
same way and repaired. Each repaired reading is pinned by `tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r3.py` (documents built in

(`studio.gi.detect.status_for_span`) of every finding's text was checked. The engine label stays `cpt-studio-ingest@4`:
no reader of this branch was ever installed.
