| N-1 | the notice counted 'characters in a font ... this server cannot read' where it counts what stands in their place: 15 for a 30-character line under a CMap pypdf does not implement | (mojibake, unnamed) | (dropped, named without a count) | 'text in a font or encoding this server cannot read as the PDF encodes it; 15 characters stand in its place as �, or as the unread mark [?] in a box' | **repaired**: the count is of what stands in the text's place, never of the page's glyphs |

What the reader does now, where it differs from round 2's rules above (which this replaces where they disagree):

- **Text that cannot be read keeps its place.** The page walk reads a text-showing operator itself where pypdf cannot
  read all of it as the PDF encodes it (`pdfcontent._TextPlacement.transcribed`): each code pypdf can read, as pypdf
  reads it; in place of each character it cannot read the reader's unread character, as many as pypdf would have
  written there where the string's codes cannot be told apart. What it read is drawn, for extraction, in a per-walk
  `pdffonts.Transcript` font (Identity-H, zero-wide codes for the text, power-of-two-wide codes that stand for
  nothing), as wide as pypdf measures the operator, so pypdf spaces the text around it as before and the words either
  side never close up. The unread character is the noncharacter U+10FFFE, which pypdf reads left to right (outside
  both its right-to-left and its neutral ranges, as the installed reader's U+FFFD and mojibake were: it ends any
  right-to-left run, so it never reverses what follows); a space the walk reads is U+10FFFF, which pypdf reads as
  neutral (one of its custom special characters), as the space it stands for. The page reader writes the unread
  character as U+FFFD, or as the '?' of the unread mark where it stands alone in a typed box, and names it: "Page N has
  text in a font or encoding this server cannot read as the PDF encodes it; K characters stand in its place as �, or
  as the unread mark [?] in a box, so check the original." (partial; K counts what stands in the text's place). That
  notice is separate from the invalid-text notice, and a failed result carries neither. U+FFFD written so is not
  readable text: a page of nothing else has no usable text layer and goes to OCR or is named. The same holds in an
  appearance's text, which is read with its U+FFFD (round 2 left such an annotation wholly unread); an appearance only
  compared with a stored value or /Contents is named unreadable, as before.
- **Which text that is**: a string a composite font's CMap cannot decode, a CMap pypdf does not implement, an embedded
  CMap the reader cannot read (with a ToUnicode, one of one-byte or of two-byte codes is read), a font pypdf cannot
  build or the resources do not hold, and (new) a glyph-index font, whose glyph indices pypdf reads as UTF-16BE.
  Glyphs a font itself gives no character (a private-use, control or empty ToUnicode entry, an unmapped symbol glyph)
  are counted and dropped, as installed (below).
- **A lone glyph that cannot be read at the start of a checklist row** (the first thing on its line, one glyph, then
  padding and its row's text; or alone on its line with the next line starting just right of it on its baseline, as
  pypdf writes ReportLab's separately drawn strings) is the unread mark '[?]' on its item's line: held.
- **Codes.** A string in a codec font whose only failing codes are whole two-byte codes the codec lacks is read code by
  code: a gap is read by the CMap's own wider reading (GB 18030 for the GBK CMaps, whose two-byte area is GBK's and
  more) or named alone (ETen-B5's extensions, which no codec here reads as the CMap does). A string cut inside a code,
  holding a byte no code starts with, or an odd-length UTF-16BE string (one-byte text in a two-byte font) is not read
  code by code: reading its pairs would be mojibake. An embedded CMap is read in one pass; one of one-byte codes with a
  ToUnicode is read one byte a code by that ToUnicode (a code it does not give is unread), with its widths by code.
- **Stored values.** A value or /Contents that names no character once squeezed (nothing, or only a NUL) shows
  nothing: the appearance is read; a NUL that ends a value is a C string's terminator, not a glyph.
- **Cost.** What font dictionaries share is worked out once per shared object (`FontReadings.cache`): an embedded
  CMap's reading, a descendant's widths matched to it, whether a copy builds, a marked ToUnicode; a widget asks the
  unread operators' bands by bisection; a field's value is read and squeezed once a field, an appearance read once a
  page, and a field that shows other text than its value is named once, however many widgets show it; operators the
  walk transcribes that draw the same text as wide share their operations.

Each repaired bound is also pinned in process by a doubling test in `tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r3.py` (`CostTests`:
twice the input costs at most about twice as much, both timed in turn): an embedded CMap of unclosed sections, one
CMap shared by many font dictionaries, one marked ToUnicode shared by many, widgets beside unread operators, a long
value shown by many widgets, lone marks beside many readings, and transcription itself (3,000 and 6,000 unread
operators hold three transcription operations between them).

Round 1's COST-1 glyph-index shapes (50 TJ of 10,000 glyph-index strings beside one readable line, and its control
with a short /BaseFont: 500,000 and 450,000 glyphs on one page) change reading: their glyph-index text is U+FFFD in
its place (GI-1), so the page's text (500,056 and 450,056 characters) is over the 400,000-character limit and the
result fails, where the installed reader and round 2 returned the readable line at ok and named the glyphs as not
mapping. Round 1's finding there, the time (240 s), stays repaired (10.2 s and 9.5 s). It is the one change of reading
on a round-1 or round-2 finding input, and it is the rule's: the installed reader returned one line of what the page
draws, at ok. It is a capability the owner may weigh against the rule; reverting GI-1 is `decode_plan` returning True
for a glyph-index font and `pdf._decoder` returning None for its glyphs.

### Round 4: the refutation of round 3, and its repair (2026-11-01; interpretation 66)

A three-lens delta refutation of round 3's reader (`058e8144f`: wrong text and boxes, capability on ordinary files,
cost and limits) filed 8 findings, 3 of them major and none blocking. Each was reproduced here through the real
extraction child, the installed reader (`origin/main` 06e6d204e, pypdf 6.21.2) beside round 3's (pypdf 6.21.4), on the
lenses' own inputs, with OCR unavailable and with stand-in OCR tools: all reproduce as reported. They are repaired under
round 3's rule, which stands (**text that cannot be read is never deleted**), and two more: **no reading falls below
the installed reader's where it read the source right** (a placeholder or a hold where it read exact text is a
regression), and **the unread mark '[?]' is written only on a checklist row**, never where a check box's state governs
the spot, a box glyph's string of two characters being a box as one of one character is. Measuring every finding input
of rounds 1 to 3 against the first of them found three more readings below the installed reader's (NR-1 to NR-3
below), and measuring that repair on fillable and printed option rows and on procedure lists found three more
(NR-4 to NR-6); each was confirmed the same way and repaired. Each repaired reading is pinned by
`tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r4.py` (documents built in `tests/support/pypdf_shapes.py`; the cost bound by a count of
what is read and a doubling test), and routing's reading (`studio.gi.detect.status_for_span`) of every finding's text
was checked. The engine label stays `cpt-studio-ingest@4`: no reader of this branch was ever installed.

What the reader does now, where it differs from round 3's rules above (which this replaces where they disagree):

- **Codes are cut by the CMap's code space.** Every predefined CMap's code space is Adobe's, as pdf.js 4.10.38 reads
  it from its copy of Adobe's CMap resources (`pdffonts.PREDEFINED_CODE_SPACES`, 143 names in 23 code spaces; a code of
  a range's length lies in it when each byte lies between the range's first and last code's bytes at its place); an
  embedded CMap's is its own codespacerange sections and the code space of the predefined CMap it uses. A string is
  cut into codes as a CMap cuts it: at each byte the shortest range the bytes there lie in, or one byte that starts
  no code (pdf.js reads one byte there too). Under a codec (a CMap pypdf implements), a string the codec cannot decode
  whole is read code by code: each code by the codec or by the CMap's wider reading, a code cut short, a byte that
  starts no code or a code no codec reads unread; only an odd-length UTF-16BE string still cannot be told apart.
- **A CMap pypdf splits otherwise than the CMap does** (`pdffonts.Codes`), with a ToUnicode: a predefined CMap pypdf
  does not implement (two bytes a code to pypdf, as UTF-16BE), an embedded CMap of mixed code lengths or that uses a
  predefined one (one byte a code), an embedded CMap that cannot be read (malformed, using a CMap other than a
  predefined one, or past the document's bound) by the code space its ToUnicode declares (PDF 32000-1 9.10.3 requires it
  to be consistent with the encoding), and /Identity (as Identity-H). A string whose codes pypdf splits as the CMap
  does, each given by the ToUnicode (and, as UTF-16BE, none pairing with the next into one character), is read as pypdf
  reads it; any other code by code by the ToUnicode, a code it does not give unread. With no ToUnicode such text cannot
  be read, as before.
- **A one-byte embedded CMap's missing codes**: where every code its ToUnicode gives is its own character, a code it
  does not give is read as pypdf reads it, its own ASCII character (pypdf's StandardEncoding agrees); otherwise unread.
- **The unread mark on a checklist row.** One or two characters that cannot be read, alone where a row's box stands (the
  first thing on its line, then padding and the row's text; or alone on its line, the next line starting just right of
  it on its baseline), are the unread mark '[?]' only where the row is a checklist row: its glyph is a box shape (an
  unchecked or checked box glyph or a check mark, not the filled square nor a white square typed as a bullet as often,
  read for this alone by the codec of the character set its predefined CMap encodes, `pdffonts.box_glyphs`); or a row of
  its list shows, at its start, a box glyph that can be read, a check box's glyph written there, or a glyph that is a
  box shape (an item's options later in its row are that row's own: NR-6; a list: consecutive rows, a row whose glyph
  pypdf wrote on a line of its own taking its label's line, blank lines aside; a heading ends it); or an unchecked check
  box lies on its row, tied to nothing there (a checked one agrees with its item read as listed: NR-5). Never where
  boxes the row shows of its own govern it: a check box tied to the row's label, or the item's options (a printed box or
  a check box's glyph on its line or its label's: '� Diabetes: ☐ Yes ☒ No', NR-4), a box shape included. Anything else
  (a bullet in a font that cannot be read) is U+FFFD and its item reads as listed, as the installed reader's mojibake
  left it. A glyph-index glyph names nothing, so a glyph-index box is a box only by its list or a check box.
- **Under a check box**: text that cannot be read lying under a check box (within its width, give or take BOX_SLACK)
  is its printed box: it neither makes the box unsure as text beside it would, nor is it the box's label to its left.
- **Beside a check box**: text that cannot be read on a check box's row makes an unchecked box unsure ('[?]') only where
  it may be the box's label (left of it, no piece of text that can be read starting between them) or lies right of it
  (before the piece the box is tied to, or running on from that piece); a bullet before an item whose options the boxes
  are is neither (round 2's rule counted any such text on the row: NR-4).
- **The labelled block stays.** A page read with OCR keeps its labelled block after the OCR text (form-field values
  and annotation text, read without the page's fonts), and so does a page with no usable layer and no OCR, whose
  layer and block are returned (partial, named) where the document would otherwise fail with no text; the form-field
  and annotation notices are given for such a page, and the notices about the block's own text.
- **Cost.** A document reads at most `MAX_DOCUMENT_CMAP_BYTES` (4 MiB) of embedded CMaps, each counted once however
  often it is read (each at most 1 MiB, as before); past it a font whose CMap was not read is read by its ToUnicode's
  code space (or, with none declared there, is text that cannot be read). A code space is one regular expression: a
  string costs one pass. Whether an operator's glyphs are a box shape is worked out once per font and strings, and only
  for an operator of at most 16 bytes whose text could not be read. Whether text that cannot be read may be a check
  box's label costs a bisection a row of the box (the nearest piece that can be read on its left, `pdf._I4_Readable`),
  beside the bounded scan of its row's unread text it always made.

Ordinary files, re-measured on this reader's committed tree (`5f13484ba`): the 935 files of round 3's comparison read
through the real child by the installed reader on 6.21.2 and this reader on 6.21.4, with OCR unavailable and with
stand-in OCR tools. 834 read identically and 101 differ: the same 101 files as round 3, which this reader reads exactly
as round 3's does (all 935). Every difference was judged by the source:

| files | installed (6.21.2) | this reader | by the source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 49 whose font lacks a glyph the text asked for (round 3's 49) | the same text, the .notdef glyphs dropped unnamed (one file: one counted) | the same text, and 'N characters that do not map to text' | as rounds 2 and 3: the notice is right; the text is identical |
| 42 ReportLab CIDFont files under a CMap pypdf does not implement, with no ToUnicode | the CID line as CJK mojibake, at ok | 15 U+FFFD in its place, named (partial) | the installed reading is wrong; with no ToUnicode no code can be read here (pdf.js reads it with its own CMap files) |
| 6 pypdf fills flattened over pdfkit's DejaVu form | partial, the flattened line missing ('damaged') | 7, 3 or 14 U+FFFD in its place, named | pdf.js reads mojibake there: held in place |
| 2 MuPDF conversions in a Unifont glyph-index font | ok, three lines dropped (122 glyphs named as not mapping) | 122 U+FFFD in their place, named | held in place (GI-1) |
| 2 fpdf2 CID-keyed CFF files using more than 255 glyphs | ok, mojibake from code 0x100 on | ok, every character exact | the installed reading is wrong |

None is a placeholder or a hold where the installed reader read exact text: no regression. Time over the 935 files with
OCR unavailable: this reader 654 s (the longest file 3.7 s), four files at a time beside this round's gates and cost
pass (load 17.02 on 14 cores at the end); the installed reader 862 s in round 3's pass, under another load, so only the
peak memory compares: the child's peak resident memory was at most 137.9 MiB against 138.8 MiB.

Every finding input of rounds 1 to 3, re-measured the same way (round 1's 90 text inputs, round 2's 34, round 3's own 7,
the 25 check-box documents and the round-3 refutation's 47 meaning and 52 capability inputs differ from the installed
reader in 49, 15, 6, 0, 32 and 17; on `5f13484ba` every one reads as on the first repair's last commit, `737cce0c1`).
Each difference from the installed reader was judged by the source; the ones a mechanical check flags (U+FFFD or '[?]'
where the installed line held neither, or a routing reading the installed reader had and this reader changes) are of
these kinds, none a reading below the installed reader's where it read the source right:

- a checklist whose box glyphs cannot be read but are box shapes (DROP-BOX-1's ReportLab input, round 3's own rows, the
  meaning lens's /EUC-H variants): held, where the installed reader read every unchecked item as affirmed;
- a glyph that names no character alone in brackets (round 1's F3, F4 and F5 inputs: a NUL, an empty or U+FFFE ToUnicode
  entry, '/u20', a private-use code): '[?]', held, where the installed reader read '[]' (negated) or '[U+FFFE]'
  (affirmed), as round 1 ruled;
- glyph-index text (round 3's GI-1 inputs, the bullets and boxes of the meaning lens): U+FFFD in place where the
  installed reader dropped it ('Biopsy was taken.' for a negation; a stamp's 'QRW'); readings otherwise unchanged;
- a right-to-left font whose ToUnicode lacks a code that is not its own character ('ז והד גבא�', the installed 'h' a
  StandardEncoding guess the glyph does not show);
- pages the installed reader sent to OCR (its layer unusable to it) whose layer this reader reads;
- text shown after a Q that restored a graphics state with no font (round 1's `font-set-inside-q`): U+FFFD in place,
  named, where the installed reader returned pypdf's reading in its default font. The PDF sets no font for that text
  and pdf.js draws none of it, so it is judged not to be text the source shows (round 3's judgement, kept); it is the
  one such reading, and a capability the owner may weigh against it.

Against round 3's reader, the finding inputs of rounds 1 and 2, round 3's own inputs and the 25 check-box documents read
identically; the round-3 refutation's inputs differ as the findings table says (the meaning lens's 47 in 22, the
capability lens's 52 in 29: each a repair above). Round 1's two cut-string appearance shapes under codec CMaps
(90ms-RKSJ-H, GBK-EUC-H) now read their codes before the cut, 'あ�' and '啊�', the installed reader's text; round 1's
test of them is updated, as is round 3's of a cut string (the codes are read, the cut code named) and of a code a
ToUnicode of its own characters does not give (read as pypdf reads it). The pinned check-box documents of round 3
(`tests/test_repairs_gi4_pypdf_r3.py`, DROP-BOX-1) read as before: their box glyphs are box shapes.

The repair measured on its own ground (NR-4 to NR-6): 32 synthetic documents (`inputs/r4-nr456.list` in the round's
scratch: intake rows whose glyph at the start is an /EUC-H bullet, a glyph-index bullet, an /EUC-H box or a readable
dash, before check boxes tied to 'Yes' and 'No', before printed options, or before a check box tied to nothing at the
row's end or over the glyph, named for its item or not; and procedure lists whose last row shows a box after its item,
or whose second item's options are boxes, printed or check boxes), each read through the real child by the installed
reader, round 3's, the first repair's last commit (`737cce0c1`) and this reader, in both modes. Round 3 held items the
installed reader read in 16 of them, the first repair in 14; this reader reads 26 exactly as the installed reader does.
Of the other 6, four (`over_euc`, `right_euc`, `rightnamed_euc`, `rightnamed_gi`) hold every row where the installed
reader held every row, and now read each checked row right (historical); the last two are the rows of a check box tied
to nothing beside a glyph-index bullet (`right_gi`, `over_gi`): their checked rows read right where the installed reader
held them, and their unchecked rows are held where the installed reader, having dropped the glyph, let routing's
field-line rule read them negated (set out below).

The new bound is pinned in process (`ManyCMapTests`): with 12 and with 24 fonts each carrying a 900 KiB embedded CMap,
the bytes of embedded CMap read are equal and within `MAX_DOCUMENT_CMAP_BYTES` and one CMap, and twice the fonts cost at
most about twice the time; past the bound each font is read by its ToUnicode's code space, and with four small ones each
is read through its CMap. NR-4's label rule is pinned the same way (`OptionRowTests`): twice the rows of check boxes
beside text that cannot be read cost about twice the time.

The gates, run as `scripts/ci/verify-cpt-studio.sh` runs them (without `pip_audit`, which queries a network service), on
a git archive of the committed tree `5f13484ba` under pypdf 6.21.4, on the disposable test database on 127.0.0.1:55461
only (an archive, so the worktree was not read while they ran): the system and migration checks pass; the Django suite
ran 3,701 tests, 3 skipped, all passing (2,584 s, under a host load of 10 to 28 on 14 cores, the corpus and cost passes
running beside it); the ops (211 tests, `test_install` and `test_verify_downloads` among those passing), verification
(37, `test_generate` among them) and browser-verifier (61 passing, none failing, the named test among them) suites pass.
The round's first repair ran 3,694 tests the same way on `a26d21cfd` and 3,695 on `737cce0c1`, all passing.

Each of the 25 mutations, each reverting one repair of this round on a copy of the committed tree (`5f13484ba`; applied
and checked present in the copy; the worktree's `studio/` and `tests/` and the archive hashed before and after:
untouched), was killed by the round's tests: a box glyph that cannot be read is one character only; no glyph is known as
a box shape; every lone glyph is a box (no checklist row needed); a check box tied to the row does not govern it, nor
any box on its line; text under a check box is text beside it; a glyph under a check box is its label; a string with a
byte outside the code space is unread whole; 0x80 under ETen-B5-H is a lead byte; OCR text replaces the form block; an
unread page's form block is no text; a predefined CMap pypdf does not implement is unread, ToUnicode or not; /Identity
is no CMap; an embedded CMap of mixed code lengths, or one that uses another, is not read; no bound on the embedded
CMaps a document reads; no code guessed from a ToUnicode of its own characters; codes UTF-16BE pairs into one character
are read whole; a mixed embedded CMap's two-byte codes are not read by its ToUnicode; a list of boxes runs on over a
heading; an embedded CMap that cannot be read is not read by its ToUnicode's code space; a row's own boxes do not govern
it; any text that cannot be read on a box's row makes it unsure; text left of a box may be its label whatever can be
read between them; a row whose label is the next line's takes no boxes from that line; a checked check box tied to
nothing makes its row a checklist row; a box anywhere on a row shows its list a box. Unmutated, the 27 tests they name
pass. Three needed a second run, said here so the record is whole: the fourth's anchor was taken before the list rule
was restructured and did not apply (the harness refused it, 0 occurrences of 1), and run again on its new text it was
killed; the eighteenth's first form also left the page's last list unclosed, so no list gave any row context and the
heading test, which asks only that no mark is written, could not tell it from the rule: it survived its own run, and run
against the glyph-index boxes' test, which wants marks from a list that ends the page, it is killed; its form that
changes the rule alone was killed. And on `899114881` (NR-4's repair) the fourth survived: a check box tied to the row's
label writes its glyph on the row, which makes it one of the row's own boxes, so the guard it removed decided nothing.
The guard was removed (`a4b9997ba`, no reading changed) and the fourth re-pointed at the own-boxes rule's line, where it
is killed.

Remaining after round 4, each read as said (round 3's table above stands where this does not replace it):

| input | output | why |
|---|---|---|
| a checklist whose boxes are glyph indices (or in an embedded CMap with no ToUnicode, or a font the resources do not hold) and no row of which shows a box that can be read or a check box | U+FFFD in each box's place, the items as listed (affirmed), as the installed reader left them (the glyph dropped), named | such a glyph names nothing, so it cannot be told from a bullet in a procedure list, which must read as listed; a box is read only by its shape (a predefined CMap's character set), its list or a check box |
| the filled square '■' (or a white square typed as a bullet) alone, its shape read | a box only in a list that shows another box, else U+FFFD and the item as listed | a bullet as often as a box, as routing reads it typed |
| a box glyph after its label, or a box of three or more characters to pypdf | U+FFFD, not the mark | only one or two characters at a row's start are taken for its box |
| a box glyph under CNS1-H, CNS2-H or CNS-EUC-H (CNS 11643), or Hankaku, Hiragana, Katakana, Roman or WP-Symbol | read as a glyph-index box is: a box only by its list or a check box | no codec here encodes those character sets, so its shape is not read |
| an unchecked check box on a row, tied to nothing there (its label left of it with nothing after it, or beyond reach of the printed box it lies over), on a row that starts with a glyph that cannot be read | the glyph '[?]': the row held (beside a checked one the glyph is U+FFFD and the item reads as listed, which agrees with it: NR-5) | the check box's state is not written on the row, and routing's field-line rule reads it from the form block only for a row that starts with its label. The installed reader dropped a glyph-index glyph and so let that rule read the state (Diabetes negated where unchecked); kept, as round 3's rule requires, the glyph stops it, and as U+FFFD it would leave the item to read as listed (affirmed): held, and set out for the owner |
| a glyph that cannot be read at the start of a row that shows boxes of its own (the item's options, or a second column's box) | U+FFFD, not the mark: the row's items read by the boxes it shows, as routing read them beside the installed reader's mojibake | those boxes govern the row (NR-4); a second column's box is read by routing as governing its line, as it was beside the installed reader |
| text in a CMap pypdf does not implement with no ToUnicode | as many U+FFFD as pypdf would have written (two bytes a character), named | the codes are cut by the code space for reading, but the count follows pypdf's reading, as round 3's did |
| a one-byte code pdf.js reads that the codec cannot decode alone (GBpc-EUC-H's 0xFD-0xFF as © ™ …, 0x80 under the GB and Big Five CMaps) | U+FFFD, named (the installed reader's reading) | no codec here reads them; pdf.js uses Adobe's CMap and its CID to Unicode table |
| an embedded CMap that cannot be read (malformed, using a CMap other than a predefined one, or past the document's 4 MiB) whose font has no ToUnicode or one that declares no code space | its text unread, named | only an embedded CMap or its ToUnicode's code space tells its codes apart |
| /Encoding /Identity with no ToUnicode | its text unread, named | it names no CMap: only a ToUnicode gives its codes text |
| text shown with no font in effect (a font set inside q/Q) | U+FFFD, named | see above: the owner's call |
